國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數初值由 58.4 降至 57.6，企業活動仍強勁擴
張，內需及出口帶動訂單與就業成長；惟油價、關稅及供應鏈瓶頸推升
成本，售價漲幅創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨壓力持續升溫，後續須
關注聯準會政策動向。 
⚫ 9 月美國住宅市場續顯疲弱，建築許可及新屋開工分別年減 3.0%及
7.4%，反映住宅投資動能放緩。儘管多戶住宅許可與完工數回升，但整
體營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率、住房負擔能力及營建成本
對市場復甦的影響。 
⚫ 密西根大學公布之 10 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.0，月減 0.2%、年
減 10.4%，顯示民眾對目前經濟環境仍缺乏信心，雖然對未來景氣的看
法略有改善，但高物價與借貸成本仍讓消費者感到壓力。此外，未來一
年及長期通膨預期分別升至 4.7%及 3.5%，反映民眾仍擔心物價持續上
漲。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月 23 日川普宣布調整加拿大商品 50%關稅清單，10 月 30 日生效，
豁免部分紙製品、機械及包裝材料，並新增地毯、油漆等課稅項目。外
媒認為此舉與期中選舉有關，有助降低部分企業成本，但美加貿易摩擦
仍未化解。 
短評 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合產出指數雖略為下滑，但仍維持高檔，顯示內需與就業持
續支撐企業活動擴張。然而，消費者信心仍處低檔，反映高物價與借貸
成本持續壓抑購買意願，儘管民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但新屋
開工及建築許可同步下滑，顯示消費與住宅投資前景仍面臨挑戰。另一
方面，美國調整部分加拿大商品關稅，雖有助減輕部分企業進口成本，
但雙邊貿易摩擦尚未化解，政策不確定性仍存。

法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，服務業、製 造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐洲議會代表團於 10 月 27 日至 29 日訪問中國，料將討論稀土及綠能
技術的依賴、貿易失衡，以及歐洲企業在中國面臨的不平等待遇問題。 
⚫ 法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大，各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日
再度發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工。 
短評 
⚫ 法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革，也難以快速推出安撫反對派
的妥協法案或者公共支出。部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延
續至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。 
⚫ 市場預期歐洲央行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場。 
日 
本 
貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月出口年增 16.7%。 
⚫ 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%。 
⚫ 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3。 
短評 
⚫ 出口維持強勁、服務需求亦持續擴張，惟中東局勢推升能源進口成本，
使貿易逆差擴大，並加重國內通膨壓力。隨成本上漲逐步由商品擴散至
服務價格，通膨黏著性可能進一步提高，為日本央行貨幣政策正常化提
供支撐；另一方面，原物料供應受限及投入成本居高不下，已開始影響
製造業動能與企業信心，後續仍須關注高成本環境對經濟成長的制約。 
中 
國 
第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
經濟

⚫ 1-9 月累計零售額年增率持平為 1.1%。 
⚫ 1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2% 
⚫ 1-9 月房地產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%。

生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%。

事件

品、家具及機械等產品，不再僅限電動車。 
⚫ 中國銀行成功發行市場首筆掛鉤 DR001 基準利率的「三農」專項金融

外銷訂單創新高，產業景氣冷熱不均 
經濟 ⚫ 9 月外銷訂單達 1,142.1 億美元，年增 62.4%。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.27 
一、 美國—企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
標普全球(S&P Global)公布，2026 年 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數(Composite 
PMI Output Index)初值由上月的 58.4 降至 57.6，其中服務業採購經理人指數
(Services PMI)由 58.8 降至 57.9，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)則由
55.9 微升至 56.0，創 2022 年 5 月以來新高，企業活動雖略有放緩，但仍維持強
勁擴張。需求方面，內需穩健及出口改善帶動整體新訂單增速創逾 4 年半新高，
商品出口訂單亦為 2025 年 6 月以來首次增加；企業持續擴增人力，整體就業增
速為 2022 年 6 月以來次高。另一方面，未完成訂單增加、供應商交貨延遲加劇，
加上油價上漲及關稅推升能源、運輸與原物料成本，使企業投入成本維持高檔，
帶動售價漲幅升至 2022 年 8 月以來最高。儘管企業對未來一年營運前景的樂觀
程度略降，但仍處於今年相對高檔。整體而言，美國經濟在內外需及就業支撐下
維持擴張，惟供應鏈瓶頸與成本上升加劇通膨壓力，後續仍須關注物價走勢及聯
準會貨幣政策動向。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 10 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.0，略低於
上月的 48.1，月減 0.2%、年減 10.4%，並較今年 2 月下降約 15%，顯示消費者信
心持續處於低檔。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 50.9 降至 46.7，月減 8.3%、
年減 20.3%；消費者預期指數則由 46.3 回升至 48.8，月增 5.4%、年減 3.0%，反
映民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但整體信心仍偏弱。調查指出，高利率及購
買負擔壓力使耐久財購買條件下降 18%，拖累當前經濟狀況指數；未來一年企業
景氣預期雖改善約 14%，但仍低於去年同期 16%。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨
預期由 4.6%升至 4.7%，長期通膨預期亦由 3.4%升至 3.5%，雙雙連續第二個月攀
升，創今年 5 月以來最高水準。密西根大學指出，2025 年關稅政策衝擊及 2026
年伊朗衝突引發的能源價格上漲，使民眾通膨預期再度升溫，且長期預期已明顯
高於 2019 年及 2024 年水準。整體而言，10 月美國消費者信心大致持平，但高物
價與借貸成本仍壓抑購買意願，加上短期及長期通膨預期持續上升，可能增加物
價回穩的難度，後續仍須關注能源價格、家庭消費及通膨預期變化對聯準會貨幣
政策的影響。

2026 年 9 月美國住宅市場建築許可年化總數降至 140.0 萬戶，月減 0.2%、年
減 3.0%，其中單戶住宅建築許可降至 84.5 萬戶，月減 4.0%、年減 4.8%，惟 5 戶
以上多戶住宅建築許可增至 50.3 萬戶，月增 5.9%、年增 1.0%，顯示不同住宅類
型開發規劃表現分歧。新屋開工年化數降至 122.1 萬戶，月減 5.2%、年減 7.4%，
其中單戶住宅開工 85.9 萬戶，較去年同期仍增 2.6%，但多戶住宅開工降至 34.9
萬戶，年減 25.9%，拖累整體營建動能。施工方面，在建住宅維持 128.0 萬戶，與
上月持平但較去年減少 3.3%；完工年化數則回升至 119.2 萬戶，月增 2.2%，主要
受多戶住宅完工月增 14.9%帶動，惟整體仍較去年同期減少 14.0%。整體而言，
美國住宅市場呈現建築許可及新屋開工下滑、在建規模持平及完工略有回升的態
勢，反映住宅營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率走勢、住房負擔能力及營
建成本變化對住宅市場復甦的影響。 
美國總統川普 10 月 23 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 338 條簽署公告，調整部
分加拿大商品加徵 50%關稅的適用範圍，並自 10 月 30 日起生效。此次調整取消
膠合板、部分紙製品、機械與冷凍設備、包裝用木箱及紙箱、新鮮與乾燥海藻等
商品的額外關稅，同時新增閥門、地毯、油漆、洗髮精、可可粉、木製棺材、空
調設備及獨木舟等課稅項目，呈現局部鬆綁與新增課稅並行的政策方向。白宮表
示，此舉旨在減輕加拿大貿易措施對美國企業造成的負擔，並維護國內產業利益。
由於緬因州農業及製造業高度依賴加拿大進口物資，共和黨參議員柯林斯(Susan 
Collins)積極向白宮爭取關稅豁免，外媒亦將此次調整與 11 月 3 日期中選舉連結。
整體而言，新措施有助降低部分美國企業的進口成本，但美加貿易摩擦尚未完全
化解，後續仍須關注雙邊談判及關稅政策對供應鏈的影響。 
二、歐元區—法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，抵銷上月的強勁
增幅，但為連續五個月高於 50 的榮枯線，主要係受到法國大規模抗議活動的影
響，使法國綜合 PMI 下降 2.7 點至 48.4；此外，德國綜合 PMI 下降 1 點至 52.8，
亦反映近期通膨壓力增加，使民眾傾向保守消費。若以產業區分，歐元區服務業、
製造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9；惟德國製造業 PMI 上升至相當強勁
的 54.2，反映德國政府擴張軍備投資以及基礎建設之需求，持續推動製造業生產
動能。

6

法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大，各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日再度
發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工，抗議主要訴求包括改
善公共服務品質及凍結物價上漲。然而，在財政壓力巨大且國會缺乏穩定執政多
數的情況下，法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革，也難以快速推出安撫反
對派的妥協法案或者公共支出。故部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延續
至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。受法國局勢影響，近期歐元區各國殖利率普遍
呈現上升趨勢，並對景氣產生暫時性的抑制效果。在此背景下，市場預期歐洲央
行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場，後續關注歐洲央行是否將透過政策
訊號或流動性工具，為平息法國主權債務利率而採取行動。 
三、日本—貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
 9 月出口雖維持強勁，受半導體製造設備及汽車出口增加所帶動，出口額年增
16.7%；惟進口增幅更為顯著，9 月進口額年增 18.9%，其中原油及液化天然氣進
口額分別大增 51.1%及 39.2%，主要反映中東局勢推升能源價格，以及日本尋求
替代能源供應來源的影響。由於進口額高於出口額，9 月貿易逆差達 5,365 億日
圓，為連續第 5 個月呈現逆差；累計 2026 上半財年（4 至 9 月）貿易逆差亦較上
年同期擴大 98.8%至 2.82 兆日圓。整體而言，出口並未明顯轉弱，近期貿易逆差
擴大主要反映能源進口成本攀升，使進口增幅持續高於出口。 
 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%，即使政府透過能源及教育補貼等措
施抑制物價，通膨仍高於 2%，顯示實際通膨動能更為強勁。從物價結構來看，石
油、原物料及工資成本上升已逐步傳導至終端價格，漲價範圍由家具、醫療用品
及休閒用品等商品，進一步擴散至房屋維護、公共運輸、住宿及外食等服務項目，
顯示企業成本轉嫁範圍逐步擴大，通膨壓力亦由商品向服務領域擴散。隨通膨持
續高於 2%且漲價範圍擴大，亦為日本央行進一步推動貨幣政策正常化提供支撐。 
 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3，顯示經濟仍維持穩定擴張，其中製造業 PMI 下
降 0.8 點至 53.3，產出指數亦降至 3 月以來最低，主要反映新訂單及出口訂單成
長放緩，加上稀土金屬等原料供應受限，對生產形成一定壓力，惟生產與訂單仍
處擴張區間，尚未顯示製造業景氣明顯轉弱；服務業 PMI 回升 0.7 點至 52.0，新
業務及出口訂單同步改善，顯示服務需求仍具韌性，為支撐景氣的重要動能。另
一方面，能源、薪資及原物料成本居高不下，使投入價格壓力持續偏高，並逐漸

### Page 8

用相關內容。

7

影響企業對未來景氣的信心。 
四、中國—第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率初估值為 4.5%，較第二季的 4.3%上揚 0.2 個百分
點；累計 1-9 月 GDP 年增率則為 4.6%。第三季的二、三級產業年增率分別較第
二季上揚 0.4、0.1 個百分點至 3.4%，其中製造業、批發零售業年增率分別達 5.9%、
2.3%為主要動能，抵銷建築業年減 4.4%等拖累因素影響。整體而言，第三季 GDP
年增率上揚有部分受到中秋國慶連假落在 9 月末段(去年同期則於 10 月 1 日開
始)，致基期效果發揮部分影響，相關消費動能仍可能會在 10 月出現暫時性的回
落。 
在需求指標方面，(1)1-9 月累計零售額年增率為 1.1%，持平於 1-8 月的累計
年增率。其中 9 月零售額年增率增加 0.7 個百分點至 1.1%，部分受到新手機上市
帶動買氣，9 月通訊器材消費年增率高達 31.1%，加上中秋國慶長假帶動食品消
費年增率增加 1 個百分點至 2.1%所致；(2)1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2%，
反映供應鏈移轉、政府主導去化過剩產能等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎
縮產業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等；(3)1-9 月房地
產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%、施工面積年減幅為 12.8%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13.2%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動，9 月工業生產年增率較 8 月增加 0.3 個百分點
至 5.5%(累計 1-9 月工業生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%)。依產業別來看，9
月主要受到煤礦開採、化工、化纖等產業擴張影響，主因中秋與國慶長假前發電
業充實庫存水位，促使煤炭企業加快復產，加上油價上漲帶動相關中下游供應鏈
增產；惟高科技產品包含 IC、機器人、新能源車等年增率皆有放慢，反映去年中
秋國慶連假皆落在 10 月，使得去年 9 月工作日較多，致基期相對墊高的影響。 
展望未來，儘管政府 10 月初宣布釋放 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務結存
限額，但額度下達到資金撥付往往需要數月時間，預期對於第四季的經濟影響有
限，使今年全年經濟成長率預估將落在 4.5%附近。此外，受歐盟右派勢力崛起及
保護主義影響，歐盟對中國貿易政策可能持續趨嚴，後續仍須關注雙方談判及溝
通結果，未來若外需動能減弱，預期中國政府將持續透過發展特定高科技產業及
扶植內需來支持經濟成長動能，以減輕房市不振及貿易環境惡化的衝擊。

五、印度及東南亞—新、馬出口保持強勁增速，菲律賓央行大舉升息 
(1)印度: 10 月 HSBC 綜合採購經理人指數（PMI）初值上升 0.8 點至 56.7，其中，
製造業 PMI 略下滑 0.2 點至 54.9、服務業 PMI 則上揚 1.0 點至 56.2，皆保持穩健
擴張態勢。由於美國、英國、中東及孟加拉等地的訂單增加，帶動新訂單及出口
訂單加速成長，製造商採購庫存增速創八個月來最快、成品庫存增速亦創歷史新
高，然而聘僱指數降至今年以來最低、未完成訂單下降，反映現階段人力充足。
另一方面，投入成本漲幅連續第七個月下降並降至近一年低點，產品售價漲幅亦
明顯放緩，通膨壓力緩解，有助提升企業獲利能力及消費者購買力，並且給予印
度央行（RBI）貨幣政策空間。 
(2)新加坡：9 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 68.4%，增速史上最快，連續第
23 個月正成長，其中，人工智慧需求強勁，帶動電子產品出口躍增 107.6%；非電
子產品出口亦大幅成長 54.2%，主要由船舶和遊艇組件所帶動。按出口目的地劃
分，對香港出口激增 121.1%、對台灣、韓國出口增幅達 96%，對美出口成長 73.2%，
但對印度出口下降 5.6%。展望未來，新加坡金融管理局在 10 月的宏觀經濟評估
報告中表示，直至 2027 年人工智慧 (AI) 驅動的成長將保持相對穩定，支持未
來幾季經濟成長表現。由於基數較高以及人工智慧相關投資預計放緩，全球利率
上升可能會對新加坡主要貿易夥伴的消費和非人工智慧投資造成壓力，可能會影
響外部需求，薪資成長放緩、招聘謹慎以及運輸成本上升可能會在 2026 年下半
年對可自由支配支出和零售活動構成壓力，預計 2027 年 GDP 成長將放緩。 
(3)印尼：印尼央行（BI）10 月會議連續第四次會議維持基準利率(7 天期逆回購利
率,BI-Rate)於 5.75%不變，貸款便利利率（LFR）維持在 6.50%不變，但將隔夜存
款便利利率（DFR）調高 25 個基點至 5%，旨在提高印尼盾資產的回報率，藉此
吸引並留住外資，同時宣布分兩階段調降存款準備率，11 月 1 日起下調 1 個百分
點至 8%，12 月 1 日起進一步降至 7%，以提供銀行體系流動性。整體而言，印尼
央行爲捍衛印尼盾，在 5 至 6 月間共升息 100 個基點，同時調降外資避險成本，
並採取「資金停留愈久、優惠愈高」的機制吸引外資流入。惟近期國際指數編纂
公司 MSCI 擬將印尼股市由”新興市場”降級為”獨立市場”，引發資金外流疑慮，
央行選擇利率按兵不動，搭配降準提供銀行體系流動性，以兼顧印尼盾穩定與國
內經濟需求，未來 MSCI 檢討進展(最快 2027 年 4 月公布)及印尼盾走勢，將持續
牽動央行後續政策。

(4)菲律賓：菲律賓央行（BSP）10 月 22 日宣布大幅調升基準利率(逆回購利率)2
碼至 5.50%，為 2023 年以來最大幅度升息。央行指出， 9 月通膨率已升至 7.2%，
遠高於 2%-4%的目標區間；同時核心通膨持續攀升，顯示價格上漲已由食品與能
源擴散至整體經濟。BSP 亦擔憂中東情勢推升國際油價、運輸費率調漲，以及聖
嬰現象衝擊農業生產，進一步推高物價。此外，披索貶至歷史低點附近，增加進
口通膨風險，因此採取較激進的緊縮措施以穩定市場預期，貨幣政策重點已由支
持經濟成長轉向優先遏制通膨風險。 
(5)馬來西亞：9 月出口額年增 54.5%至 MYR2,151 億元，其中電子&電氣產品出
口大增 69.2%，持續反映 AI 帶動的強勁需求，礦產品出口亦大幅成長 56.3%，抵
銷農產品出口續跌 5.7%的影響，對台灣、美國出口大增 175.2%、89.9%、對新加
坡出口增幅亦達 50.4%。9 月進口額成長 52.7%至 MYR1,814 億元，受惠出口引
申需求，中間產品、資本貨物進口分別成長 69.6%、20.9%，消費品進口轉為正成
長 18.2%，自中國和新加坡進口分別成長 67.6%、90.7%。進出口相抵，9 月貿易
順差達 MYR337 億元、年增 64.9%。 
六、台灣—外銷訂單創新高，產業景氣冷熱不均 
9 月外銷訂單達 1,142.1 億美元，再創單月新高，年增 62.4%，主要受惠於人
工智慧、高效能運算與雲端服務等應用蓬勃發展，以及消費性電子新品陸續推出，
帶動相關供應鏈維持高檔；加上國際原物料價格上漲，推升整體接單表現。惟各
產業接單動能仍呈分化，科技產品需求強勁，傳統產業景氣則相對疲弱。經濟部
指出，雖有年底節慶補貨潮挹注，但傳統產業部分產能過剩，終端需求仍未見明
顯回升。

First Read
Accton Technology Corporation
Q4 GM to see strong recovery
Q3 GM  down 2.1pp QoQ on higher component cost
Accton reported Q326 EPS of NT$23.39, below UBS/Consensus estimates on softer
margins. Q326 sales of NT$120.7bn (up 26% QoQ) was already posted, and slightly
below UBSe as its  AI card business (Trainium 3) shifted to consignment model for certain
higher cost components from mid-August. Q326 GM of 17.6% was down 2.1ppts QoQ

due to   higher component cost and ~1.5 months of  consignment, and we believe this
should be temporary as its customer will reimburse them in the subsequent quarters. Q3
OPEX increased by  15% QoQ, primarily due to higher R&D expenses, resulted in OPM of
13.4% (-1.6ppts QoQ). We estimate data center switch accounted for ~37% of Q326
sales (vs 36% in  Q226), while accelerator card was ~50% of total sales (50% in Q226).
Q4  growth on track despite full quarter of consignment

Accton expects Q426F revenue to grow high-single digit % QoQ with potential upside
for low-teens % QoQ growth, subject to component supply (i.e. switch IC) and
customers' year-end inventory adjustment, despite a full quarter of component
consignment (vs 1.5 month in Q3). It sees robust demand from  cloud switch  with the
social media customer revenue contribution exceeded e-commerce customer from
Q326, which is likely to sustain into Q426F. It also noted the social media customer has

secured more switch chipsets from Marvell and Nvidia Spectrum, and continues to
negotiate with Broadcom for better allocation. Accton also sees better unit and content
growth for Trainium 3 as the customer ramps liquid-cooled design, which carry ~20%
higher ASP and better GM due to increased design complexity. We forecast Q426E sales
growth of 9% QoQ, while its GM should recover to 19.9% (+2.3 ppt QoQ) on more
favourable mix and its customer reimbursement on the Q3 prepaid component costs.

Aggressive capacity expansion to support mid-term  growth
Accton announced  US$226 mn (~NT$7.3 bn) new capex on 12 Nov. for capacity
expansion in Taiwan and Vietnam, as well as construction of new fab/cleanroom in
Malaysia and Singapore. It aims to fund the capex through internal cashflow, bank
loans,  and has announced a 10mn shares of rights issuance (~2% dilution) on 22 Oct.
We believe this will drive its overall capacity to grow 40-50% YoY in 2027E, supporting

overall 2027E revenue to grow ~35% YoY, driven by 1) further ramp of Trainium 3 with
higher adoption of liquid cooling, 2) sustained 800G switch demand and migration to
1.6T with new customer addition, 3) increasing contribution from Optical Switches.
Valuation: Reiterate Buy with NT$3,200 PT
We  fine tune our model and raise   2026-28E EPS by 0-2%. We retain  Buy but lower  PT to
NT$3,200 (from   NT$3,688) based on 22x  average 2027-28E PE (from 26x) on overall

market valuation compression. We believe recent concerns over switch IC  shortages and
GM pressure from higher component costs are overdone as we expect Accton's GM to
recover from Q426E. Accton will host its annual investor conference in late November.

Company Description
Founded in 1988, Accton specialises in ODM/OEM solutions for data center switches, AI
accelerator cards, enterprise/ODM switches, carrier access/telecom and others. Accton is one
of the largest vendors of white-box and OCP-compliant switches globally (by sales).

Leveraging deep expertise in high-speed switching, system integration and rapid time-to-
market, it has secured long-term partnerships with major cloud service providers.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Accton using PE methodology.
Key risks include: 1) weaker-than-expected demand for data center switches; 2) slower ramp
of 800G and 1.6T data center switches; 3) market share loss in the AI accelerator card
business; and 4) softer gross margin than expected.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

江誼蓁 
yizhen0808@yuanta.com

3Q26 EPS 低於市場預期 14%，預期 4Q26 毛利率小幅回升 
嘉澤 3Q26 營收 99.58 億元，季增 4.1%/年增 18.2%，其中伺服器營收年增
55%，惟記憶體與 PCB 缺料壓抑 CPU Socket 拉貨，原物料成本維持高檔，
加上新平台導入慢於預期，毛利率季減 0.6 個百分點至 44.8%。薪資費用高
於預期以及業外淨收益亦較上季下降，EPS 為 17.06 元，低於本中心/市場
預期 11.0%/14.4%。考量缺料延續及新平台挹注有限，下修 4Q26 營收
5.8%至 103.89 億元，季增 4.3%/年增 12.2%，毛利率估季增 0.3 個百分點
至 45.1%，EPS 下修 12.6%至 18.79 元。 
QD 擴產及伺服器平台換代帶動 2027 年營收年增 30% 
QD 產能滿載，公司目標於未來一季將產能倍增，預估 QD 占總營收比重於
2026 年 12 月達 3–5%，2027 年全年接近 10%。AMD Venice 預計自 1Q27
末加速放量，2027 年平均採用率約 20%。Intel Oak Stream 預計於 1Q27
末至 2Q27 初導入，2H27 採用率估達 10–15%。新平台有助提升 CPU 
Socket 單價及 PCIe 6.0 連接器採用率，預估 2027 年伺服器營收年增 50%，
帶動整體營收年增 30%。 
維持買進及目標價 2,200 元 
伺服器缺料及 PC 業務疲弱使營收低於前估，原物料成本居高、新平台滲透
慢於預期，加上低於公司平均毛利率的 QD 占比提高及薪資費用增加，下修
2026/2027 年 EPS 6.0%/5.4%至 74.92/106.29 元。評價方面，本中心仍看
好 2027 年 QD 擴產將擴大營收與獲利貢獻，Venice 與 Oak Stream 逐步放
量則有助改善 CPU Socket 產品組合。隨營收規模擴大，預估 2027 年營益
率由 24.5%回升至 28.3%，帶動 EPS 年增 41.9%，因此維持買進評等，以 
2027 年預估 EPS 106.29 元 21 倍本益比推得目標價 2,200 元。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 EPS 為 17.06 元，低於市場預期 14%，主因薪資費用提升以及
原物料成本居高、新平台導入進度不如預期，毛利率季減至 44.8%。 
⧫  
記憶體與 PCB 缺料持續壓抑 CPU Socket 拉貨，下修 4Q26 EPS 
12.6%至 18.79 元，預期毛利率小幅回升。 
⧫  
QD 放量與 AMD/Intel 新平台導入帶動 2027 年營收年增 30%、EPS
年增 42%至 106.29 元，維持買進及目標價 2,200 元。

### Page 2

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 11 月 16 日 Lotes 第2 頁，共 16頁
營運分析
3Q26 毛利率低於預期，薪資費用提升，EPS 低於市場預期 14%
嘉澤 3Q26 營收 99.58 億元，季增 4.1%/年增 18.2%，低於本中心/市場預期 1.7%/2.5%，其中伺服
器營收年增 55%，占總營收 53.1%，惟記憶體與 PCB 缺料持續限制客戶出貨，CPU Socket 拉貨
低於預期。原物料成本維持高檔，伺服器新平台導入慢於預期，加上部分桌機客戶因缺料改採支援
DDR4 或 DDR3 的舊平台，使低單價、低毛利產品占比偏高，毛利率季減 0.6 個百分點至 44.8%，
低於本中心/市場預期 0.7/0.9 個百分點。中國廠區自 2Q26 起調薪，使費用率年增約 0.7 個百分點。
受惠於營收規模擴大，費用率仍季減 1.6 個百分點至 21.9%，營益率升至 22.9%，營業利益季增
8.6%至 22.84 億元。業外淨收益由上季 4.95 億元降至 0.95 億元，歸屬母公司淨利季減 3.3%至

19.19 億元。EPS 為 17.06 元，季減 3.5%/年減 17.6%，低於本中心/市場預期 11.0%/14.4%。

新平台導入速度不如預期，下修 4Q26 EPS 13%至 18.79 元
嘉澤 10 月營收 32.0 億元，月減 5%/年增 12%，達本中心 4Q26 營收原估的 29%。公司預期 4Q26
營收呈低個位數季增，零組件缺料仍是主要出貨限制，因此本中心下修 4Q26 營收 5.8%至 103.89
億元，季增 4.3%/年增 12.2%。SOCAMM 等新品出貨增加有助毛利率回升，惟 AMD Venice 年底
採用率僅估 3–5%，Intel Oak Stream 預計於 1Q27 末至 2Q27 初開始導入，高針腳數 CPU Socket

對 4Q26 產品組合的挹注低於前估，加上原物料成本維持高檔，以及毛利率低於公司平均的 QD 出
貨增加，本中心下修 4Q26 毛利率 1.4 個百分點至 45.1%，季增 0.3 個百分點。預估營業利益季增
10.2%至 25.17 億元，EPS 下修 12.6%至 18.79 元，季增約 10%/年減 16.5%。

QD 擴產與伺服器平台換代帶動 2027 年營收年增 30%
本中心預估嘉澤 2027 年營收年增 30%，主要成長動能來自 QD 放量與通用伺服器平台換代。公司
預估 2027 年伺服器整機出貨量年增 10–15%，新平台導入也將推升 CPU Socket 單價，並帶動
PCIe 6.0 連接器採用率提升。AMD Venice 目前採用率不到 3%，預計自 1Q27 末加速放量，2027
年全年採用率約 20%。Intel Oak Stream 預計於 1Q27 末至 2Q27 初開始導入，2H27 採用率估達

10–15%，營收貢獻主要落在下半年。隨 PCIe 連接器與線材市占率提升，本中心預估 2026/2027
年伺服器營收分別年增 56%/50%。嘉澤另預計於 2H27 開始出貨 Arm CPU 平台的客製化 Socket。
QD 目前產能滿載，公司目標於未來一季將產能倍增。預估 QD 占總營收比重於 2026 年 12 月達
3–5%，2027 年全年接近 10%。QD 放量將稀釋整體毛利率，但新增營業費用相對有限，規模擴大
後仍可挹注營業利益及 EPS。隨伺服器新平台採用率提高及 SOCAMM 出貨增加，本中心預估
2027 年毛利率由 46.2%回升至 48.5%。NPO Socket 預計於 2027 年以總頻寬 3.2Tbps 的產品小量
出貨，占營收不到 2%，2028 年隨單價較高的 6.4Tbps 產品導入，營收貢獻將更為明顯。
非伺服器業務方面，公司預估 2027 年 PC 市場出貨量再減約 10%。本中心估嘉澤 2026/2027 年筆
電營收分別年減 17%/4%，桌機營收年減 30%/年增 1%;策略客戶營收年增 3%/2%，車用年增

27%/15%，工業及醫療年增 22%/20%。嘉基受惠於高速線材出貨成長，營收預估年增 12%/40%。

產業概況  
2026 年 PC 出貨恐衰退近雙位數 
記憶體價格大漲，影響 PC 出貨。受到上游記憶體成本大幅上揚，PC 廠商即使轉嫁或降低規格，
均將影響消費者購買意願，因此預估 2026 年 PC(NB+DT)出貨衰退 9.6%，NB 出貨亦年減 9.7%。
2027 年 PC 及 NB 出貨量才有機會回升低個位數。 
上半年消費者提前拉貨，下半年出貨待觀察。1H26 市場預期記憶體及儲存裝置未來大漲，因此客
戶有提前拉貨現象，IDC 統計全球 PC 出貨 679 萬台，季減 16%，但年增 3%，2Q26 起 PC 步入
衰退 5%，下半年更衰退至 15~20%。

### Page 6

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 11 月 16 日 Lotes 第6 頁，共 16頁  
AI、ASIC、通用型強勢出擊，2026/2027 年美系 CSP 資本支出估年增 95%/46%  
在 ChatGPT 等 AI 應用陸續推向市場，各大業者紛紛開始研發自有超級大模型，包括 Gemini 
(Google)、Claude (Anthropic)、Llama (Meta) 等，各大品牌開始大舉投入大型語言模型(LLM)市場，
且每一個產品的智慧皆持續提高，部分消費者也陸續從 ChatGPT 陣營轉移至 Gemini 陣營，反映
出消費者開始逐漸提升對 AI 的黏著度。根據 Grand View Research 調查，2024 年全球 AI 市場營
收約 2,792 億美元，預估 2030 年市場規模超過 3 兆美元，擁有超過 30% 的年複合增長率。 
面對這爆發性的長期成長趨勢，CSP 業者(Amazon、Microsoft、Google、Meta)在法說會上一致強
調，AI 投資已轉變為一項橫跨 2026 年的結構性戰略，CSP 大廠不僅持續擴大對 GB300 等次世代
AI 伺服器的採購，更將重點放在資料中心基礎設施的長期戰略規劃，以應對 AI 運算對電力及散熱
系統強勁成長的需求，確保未來數年內擁有足夠的能源與容量來支持持續迭代的大模型部署。本中
心認為，2026 年美國主要 CSP 資本支出將年增 95%，且將有一半的成長來自於資料中心建置，代
表了資料中心的不足及未來伺服器需求。明年不僅僅是 NVIDIA 的天下，AWS、Google、Meta 
ASIC 的崛起也將是一大關鍵，其中又以 Google TPU 需求最為強勁，已經有兩個客戶預計將採購
TPU。 
另外，受惠於 AI 訓練與推論需求爆發的連帶效應，2026/2027 年通用型伺服器預估出貨量將年增
10%/9%，其中以存儲型伺服器需求最為強勁。隨著生成式 AI 應用普及和使用者數量暴增，CSP
等業者必須不斷擴充容量，且不僅限於高階 GPU 伺服器，龐大的 AI 模型訓練和推論在使用狀態中
將消耗大量的數據(DATA)，使得存儲型伺服器的需求大增。

### Page 7

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 11 月 16 日 Lotes 第7 頁，共 16頁  
ASIC 進入高速成長期，GPU 需求仍維持擴張 
從 AI 晶片預期出貨來看，GPU 與 ASIC 需求均持續成長，但 2026 年後 ASIC 成長動能明顯加速。
Nvidia GPU 出貨量預估由 2025 年 565.7 萬顆增至 2026 年 650 萬顆、2027 年 850 萬顆，年增
15%及 31%；AMD 亦由 2025 年 90 萬顆增至 2027 年 180 萬顆，顯示通用 GPU 算力需求仍維持
成長。 
相較之下，ASIC 受惠 CSP 加速自研晶片及導入規模擴大，總出貨量預估由 2025 年 380 萬顆大幅
增至 2026 年 725 萬顆、2027 年 1,360 萬顆，年增率達 91%及 88%，成長速度明顯高於 GPU。其
中 Google 仍為最大 ASIC 使用者，2027 年用量預估達 800 萬顆，AWS 亦增至 320 萬顆，
Microsoft 及 Meta 同步擴大部署。我們認為，在資料中心電力逐漸成為算力擴張瓶頸下，CSP 將
更重視每瓦運算效能及整體運算成本，ASIC 針對特定運算工作的效率優勢有望推升滲透率，惟
GPU 憑藉通用性及軟體生態系優勢，仍將維持重要地位。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 11 月 16 日 Lotes 第8 頁，共 16頁
獲利調整與股票評價
下修 2026/2027 年 EPS 6.0%/5.4%，維持買進及目標價 2,200 元
伺 服 器 供 應 鏈 缺 料 持 續 壓 抑 CPU Socket 拉 貨 ， 筆 電 及 桌 機 營 收 亦 低 於 前 估 ， 本 中 心 下 修
2026/2027 年營收 2.0%/2.6%至 392.43/510.58 億元，分別年增 16.2%/30.1%。原物料成本維持高
檔，AMD 及 Intel 新平台採用率提升慢於預期，加上 QD 營收占比提高，使毛利率改善幅度低於前
估。本中心下修 2026/2027 年毛利率 0.5/0.6 個百分點至 46.2%/48.5%。費用方面，中國廠區調薪
推升固定薪資支出，公司預估 2027 年單季營業費用約 22–25 億元。考量薪資費用高於前估，下修
2026/2027 年 EPS 6.0%/5.4%至 74.92/106.29 元，分別年增 6.8%/41.9%。

評價方面，本中心仍看好 2027 年 QD 擴產將擴大營收與獲利貢獻，Venice 與 Oak Stream 逐步放
量則有助改善 CPU Socket 產品組合。隨營收規模擴大，預估 2027 年營益率由 24.5%回升至
28.3%，帶動 EPS 年增 41.9%，因此維持買進評等，以 2027 年預估 EPS 106.29 元 21 倍本益比
推得目標價 2,200 元。

公司簡介  
嘉澤成立於 1986 年，專門生產及設計 PCIe、DDR、SATA 等連接器及 USB 連接埠，公司擁有完整的生產流程，包含設計、
加工、成型、沖壓、電鍍及自動化組裝，可提供客製化需求及標準型產品。公司為全球前三大 Server Socket (CPU 插槽)供
應商，市佔率約為 30-35%，同業為鴻海集團(鴻騰精密)及安費諾等。該產業的進入門檻高，多數產品皆已申請專利，且公
司實驗室已通過 ISO 認證，可縮短測試時間，並降低測試費用。嘉澤產品為電腦、伺服器、消費性電子產品之各式連接器
及零組件，旗下有四大事業體，分別是 PC/NB、伺服器、子公司嘉基及其他業務(包括 Type-C 連接器)。

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 嘉澤整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於低風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於中間偏前的
位置，在電子零組件行業中的公司中排名略領先於同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 嘉澤的整體曝險屬於中等水準，優於電子零組件行業的平均水準。公司較需要改善的 ESG 議
題包含公司治理、商業道德與資源使用等。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：嘉澤在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力屬中等。公司遵循適當的 ESG 揭露，
表明對投資者和公眾的強烈責任感。其 ESG 相關問題由董事會或經營團隊負責，建議將這些整合到核心業務戰略中。

附錄：重要揭露事項 
分析師聲明 
主要負責撰寫本研究報告全文或部分內容之分析師，茲針對本報告所載證券或證券發行機構，於此聲明：(1) 文中所述觀點皆準確反映其個人對各證券或
證券發行機構之看法；(2) 研究部分析師於本研究報告中所提出之特定投資建議或觀點，與其過去、現在、未來薪酬的任何部份皆無直接或間接關聯。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

貿聯-KY(3665) 
毛利重返成長軌道 
投資評等 目標價 
買進 $2,200 
前次投資建議：2026.5.13： 
「逢低買進」，目標價: $3,300

報告日期：2026.11.16 研究員：陳泂暉

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/13 收盤價： 1,520.00 毛利重返成長軌道，上調投資評等至「買進」 
目前股價 12X27(F)PER，考量： 
(1) 26Q3 公 司 營 收 為 278.54 億元，QoQ+19.6% 、
YoY+51.3%，受惠 HPC 電源需求、SPE 沉積蝕刻及次
系統提升與電氣化推進；毛利率為 32.6% ，
QoQ+1.9ppts、YoY-0.7ppts，費用率創低帶動營益率創
高；稅後淨利 42.78億元，QoQ+43.5%、YoY+62.8%；
稅後 EPS 21.93元。 
(2) 工業業務受惠工廠自動化回溫及能源、電動車等電氣化
需求推進，機器人、航太與醫療等客製化方案亦逐步貢
獻。半導體設備受惠沉積與蝕刻滲透率提升，客戶投資
看至 2030年後，帶動子系統整合與機箱組裝委外需求擴
大。 
(3) HPC受惠 AI基礎設施升級，機櫃內銅纜與機櫃外光纖將
長期共存。公司透過 XFS 強化光學封裝與量產，推進
CPO、12.8T XPO 及 Micro-VCSEL 技術；電源延伸至
Busbars、匯流排槽及水冷板等全液冷機櫃方案，結合
Interplex 機構整合並共同開發 LVDC/HVDC 架構，推升
系統級競爭力。 
(4) 預估 2027 年營收 1,636.24 億元，YoY+54.4%，受惠於
新世代平台放量；毛利率 33.6%，YoY+2.1ppts，受惠於
產品組合改善；稅後淨利 256.69 億元，YoY+74.6%；稅
後 EPS 131.58元。

貿聯目前股價位於 12X27(F)PER，位於公司過往 9-34X區間
下緣位置，考量公司持續推進光通業務，將有助於評價回升，
所以取 17X，給予「買進」投資評等， 6 個月目標價
2,200(17X2027(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 44.74%。

資料來源：CMoney；兆豐國際預估

訪談重點

全球連接線束解決方案領導者 
貿聯成立於 1996 年，總部位於加州矽谷，註冊於開曼群島，為全球連接線
束解決方案的領導廠商。產品分為工業用、電器用、車用、資訊科技與其他
五大產線。主要應用於資訊科技用線、電腦週邊設備、數據中心用線、消費
電子用線、電器用線、車用線、醫療設備用線、光通訊設備、太陽能連接裝
置、工業設備用線、半導體設備等線材、連接器等領域。公司 26Q1 產品營
收比重為: 工業用 31%、電器用 12%、車用 8%、資訊科技 48%。

HPC 與半導體設備雙引擎驅動營益率創歷史新高 
26Q3 公司營收為 278.54 億元，QoQ+19.6%、YoY+51.3%，細分產品組合
來看，HPC 高效能運算受惠於 AI 基礎設施持續投資，其中電源互連因高電
流與機櫃級配電需求強勁、旗下 XFS光學業務月營收運行率自年初以來已超
過翻倍、SPE半導體生產設備受惠於沉積與蝕刻等設備平台滲透率及次系統
承接比重提升，並持續大幅優於整體 WFE 市場、其他業務整體市場環境好
壞參半，但電氣化與自動化需求持續推進，另併購之 Interplex於 26Q3 僅計
入 7天營收，10月起完整併表並單月貢獻 6,700萬美元；毛利率為 32.6%，
QoQ+1.9ppts、YoY-0.7ppts，搭配營業費用率降至歷史新低，帶動營業利益
率創下歷史新高；稅後淨利 42.78 億元，QoQ+43.5%、YoY+62.8%；稅後
EPS 21.93元。

動能從單一零組件邁向子系統級解決方案，動能延續至 2027年後 
工業用業務方面，受惠工廠自動化需求活動持續改善，加上受惠於電氣化與
基礎設施現代化的能源、電動車及特用工程應用穩健推進，帶動整體表現持
續成長；此外，公司在機器人、航太、海事、醫療科技、半導體量測及儲能
等領域的客製化線束與整合方案，部分已成為穩定貢獻來源，部分則處於早
期認證與商業開發階段。

半導體生產設備方面，主要受惠於沉積與蝕刻設備平台滲透率與內含價值提
升，且客戶針對高複雜度製程的投資預期強勁並延伸至 2030 年以後，帶動
高複雜度組裝、子系統製造與整合、測試及機箱組裝的委外需求持續擴大。
隨著各產業客戶對供應商的要求從單一零組件轉向具備工程支援、產品認

- 3 - 
訪談速報 
證、量產準備度與穩定執行力的子系統級解決方案，結合併購 Interplex所帶
來的精密機構與機殼整合綜效，相關成長動能預期將延續至 2027 年及更長
遠的基礎設施與產能擴張週期。

機櫃內銅纜、機櫃外光纖並進，提供完整互連方案 
公司持續受惠於高速銅互連解決方案在支援更高頻寬與維持訊號完整性上的
優勢；隨著 AI系統頻寬需求、光纖密度與互連複雜度提高，光互連基礎設施
的重要性持續提升，同時銅互連在機櫃內短距離傳輸、成本與部署特性上仍
具效率優勢，因此光互連不會單向取代銅互連，而是機櫃內以銅纜為主、機
櫃外長距離傳輸以光纖為主，兩者將依頻寬、傳輸距離、功耗與封裝架構不
同而長期共存。公司透過旗下 XFS Communications 補強精密光學組裝、連
接器化製程、高密度封裝與快速量產擴張能力，並陸續於 OFC、Computex
與 OCP展出光纖方案、CPO交換器托盤及 12.8T XPO收發方案，同時推進
次世代基於 Micro-VCSEL的機櫃內擴展（Scale-up）光學互連技術與外部技
術合作，成為後續 HPC業務擴張的重要動能。

在 HPC端，AI基礎設施進入長期投資與升級週期，隨著算力密度持續攀升，
資料中心的物理基礎設施挑戰正延伸至高電流電力傳輸、散熱及系統級整
合。公司產品線已從單一電源線延伸至 Busbars、Power Whips、連接器、
匯流排槽以及水冷板、分歧管、液冷快接頭與漏液偵測等全液冷 AI 機櫃方
案，並結合併購 Interplex的精密機構與機殼整合能力強化系統級競爭力；至
於低壓直流與高壓直流新電源架構的導入管理層指出將取決於各家客戶的系
統設計、產品認證進度及資料中心電力基礎設施的就緒程度，目前公司正與
客戶密切共同開發次世代與下下世代架構，以高度彈性配合其部署排程。

新世代平台放量帶動獲利爆發 
預估 26Q4營收 339.62億元，QoQ+21.9%、YoY+71.7%，因為工業用季節
性需求回溫，以及 HPC 新平台所帶動；毛利率 32.8%，QoQ+0.2ppts、
YoY+0.9ppts，因新平台導入，因此壓抑毛利率成長幅度；稅後淨利 51.7億
元，QoQ+20.9%、YoY+88.7%；稅後 EPS 26.5元。

預估 2026年營收 1,059.61億元，YoY+48.7%，持續受惠於資料中心業務；
毛利率 31.5%，YoY-0.2ppts；稅後淨利 147.04 億元，YoY+63.3%；稅後
EPS 75.37元。

預估 2027年營收 1,636.24 億元，YoY+54.4%，受惠於新世代平台放量；毛
利率 33.6%，YoY+2.1ppts，受惠於產品組合改善；稅後淨利 256.69 億元，
YoY+74.6%；稅後 EPS 131.58元。

貿聯目前股價位於 12X27(F)PER，位於公司過往 9-34X區間下緣位置，考

操作（預期未來六個月股價漲跌幅介於-15%與+15%之間）； 逢高賣出（預期未來六個月股價跌幅介於-15%與-30%之間）；賣出
（預期未來六個月股價跌幅超過-30%）。

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com

貿聯 3Q26 財 報 優 於 預 期 ， 營 業 利 益 率 創 歷 史 新 高 ， 加 上 
Interplex 併表效益優於原先預估，為後續營運增添新亮點。展
望未來，研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，主要考量：(1) 隨 
AI 機櫃功耗持續提升，電力傳輸規格朝高電流、高壓化發展，帶
動 Power Whip、Busbar 等產品升級，單櫃電源產值可望倍增；
(2) 高速互連朝 1.6T 以上演進，AEC 與高階光纖業務接續放
量，推升資料傳輸產值；(3) Interplex 併購補足精密機構能力，
結合既有電力及高速互連布局，貿聯可望由零組件供應商逐步延
伸至機櫃次系統整合，擴大單一客戶供應範圍及長期成長商機。
研究部將 Interplex 納入預估，上修 2026、2027 年稀釋後 EPS 
至 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）及 145.24 元
（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建
置不確定性升高，可能使新平台導入與機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑
評價表現，研究部將本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍，以 2027 年 
EPS 為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至
「強力買進」。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營業利益率創歷史新高，財報優於預期 
貿聯 3Q26 營收 278.75 億元（+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY），符
合預期。其中 HPC 營收成長強勁（+30% QoQ、+61% YoY），營收
占比由 2Q26 的 42% 提升至 46%。隨 AEC 拉貨恢復正常，上半
年產線未達經濟規模的不利影響逐步消除，帶動整體毛利率回升
至 32.53%（+1.80ppt QoQ、−0.72ppt YoY）；加上營收規模擴
大，營業費用率降至 11.75%（−1.76ppt QoQ、−1.97ppt YoY），
推升營業利益率至 20.78%（+3.56ppt QoQ、+1.24ppt YoY），創
歷史新高。營業利益達 57.92 億元（+44.5% QoQ、+61.0% YoY），
優於原估的 52.50 億元。業外因匯兌損失及利息費用合計損失 
2.30 億元，稅後淨利達 42.90 億元（+43.9% QoQ、+63.2% YoY），
優於原估的 39.78 億元，稀釋後 EPS 為 20.34 元，優於原估
的 19.45 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. Interplex 併表貢獻超預期，上修 4Q26 營收與獲利 
貿聯 10 月營收 118.46 億元（+26.9% MoM、+80.0% YoY），創
歷史新高，主要受惠 Interplex 於 9 月底完成交割，10 月首
度完整併表即貢獻營收 6,700 萬美元，約為併購公告時近 12 個
月平均月營收的一倍。Interplex 為 NVIDIA NVLink Switch 機
殼主要供應商，受惠 AI 機櫃需求強勁，併表貢獻高於原先預估。
考量併購效益優於預期，加上既有業務持續成長，研究部上修 
4Q26 營收至 349.76 億元（+25.5% QoQ、+76.8% YoY，原估 
302.69 億元）。預估毛利率為 32.49%（−0.04ppt QoQ、+0.56ppt 
YoY），大致持平前季；隨營收規模擴大及 Interplex 較低費用
率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.85%，推升營業利益率至 21.64%
（+0.86ppt QoQ、+3.27ppt YoY，原估 20.0%）。預估 4Q26 稅
後淨利達 55.28 億元（+28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY），稀釋後 EPS 
由原估 22.38 元上修至 26.21 元（ +28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY）。

3. AI 機櫃供電架構升級，電源單櫃產值與市占率同步提升 
隨 AI 伺服器運算密度持續提高，機櫃功耗由 GB200 世代約 
120kW，朝 Vera Rubin 世代 200kW 以上發展，電力傳輸規格同
步升級。在既有低壓直流架構下，功耗提升帶動電流需求增加，
Power Whip 與 Busbar 須升級至更高載流規格，推升單櫃電源
產值；隨機櫃功率持續攀升，傳統 54VDC 架構面臨銅材用量、配
電損耗及機櫃空間限制，NVIDIA 已推動 800VDC 高壓直流架構，
透過提高配電電壓、減少電力轉換環節，支援未來 MW 級 AI 機
櫃需求。貿聯除受惠電源產品規格升級，於 Vera Rubin 世代的 
Busbar 市占率亦可望明顯提升，推動單櫃電源產值較 GB 世代
倍增。考量新平台相關出貨預計於 1H27 起逐季放量，研究部預
估 2027 年電源營收成長約 114% YoY；中長期隨 800VDC 架構
逐步導入，既有 Power Whip、Busbar 及高電流連接技術可望延
伸至新一代配電系統，持續擴大供應商機。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. 高速互連升級至 1.6T，AEC 與高階光纖雙軌成長 
隨 AI 資料中心傳輸頻寬由 800G 朝 1.6T 以上升級，高速互連
對訊號完整性、傳輸距離及功耗的要求持續提高。短距離傳輸仍以
銅纜具成本優勢，較長距離則由光纖滿足頻寬與訊號傳輸需求，預
期 AEC 與光互連將持續並存。AEC 方面，1.6T 新規格因材料成本
及組裝難度提高，產品單價與毛利率可望優於前代，預計於 4Q26 
至 1Q27 逐步導入量產，研究部預估 2027 年 AEC 營收成長約 
41% YoY，主要受惠高階產品占比提升。光纖方面，新富生主要供
應 AI 資料中心機櫃間傳輸所需的 MPO、MT 高密度光纖跳線及連
接器組件，受惠高速光纖互連需求成長，月營收較 2026 年初成長
逾一倍，獲利同步改善。中長期來看，貿聯亦積極布局 Optical 
Shuffle、CPO 交換器方案及 Micro-VCSEL 光互連技術，逐步由傳
統線纜組裝延伸至高階光學組件，為後續成長開拓更多商機。

圖一、貿聯-KY 1Q25～4Q27F HPC 營收結構

資料來源：統一投顧預估

5. 半導體設備需求強勁，非 HPC 業務維持穩健 
貿聯 3Q26 半導體設備營收成長 11% QoQ、70% YoY，為非 HPC 業
務主要成長動能。隨先進製程及半導體設備投資持續推進，客戶需
求由傳統線束延伸至設備模組、精密組裝及次系統整合，帶動單機
供應產值提升。貿聯已深入薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備供應鏈，並逐步拓
展至微影及檢測設備，擴大產品應用範圍。其他業務方面，工廠自
動化、醫療、能源及車用等終端需求維持穩健，提供多元營收來源。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

6. 上修 2026～2028 年獲利預估，營運槓桿與併購效益持續擴
大 
研究部將 Interplex 併表貢獻納入預估，上修貿聯 2026 年營收
至 1,069.96 億元（+50.2% YoY，原估 1,021.18 億元），毛利率
估 31.4%，稀釋後 EPS 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）。
展望 2027 年，受惠 AI 電源及高速互連業務持續成長，加上 
Interplex 全年併表，預估營收達 1,710.84 億元（+59.9% YoY，
原估 1,517.46 億元）。隨高毛利 HPC 業務占比提升，毛利率可
望升至 34.3%（原估 34.0%）；加上營收規模擴大及 Interplex 
較低費用率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.2%（原估 13.0%），推升營
業利益率至 24.1%（原估 21.0%），稀釋後 EPS 上修至 145.24 
元（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。展望 2028 年，隨 AI 機
櫃供電架構朝 800VDC 演進、高速互連規格持續升級，加上 
Interplex 受惠機櫃需求成長及精密機構件供應範圍擴大，預期將
共同帶動營運成長。研究部預估營收達 2,136.69 億元（+24.9% 
YoY），稀釋後 EPS 196.20 元（+35.1% YoY，原估 171.03 元）。

圖二、貿聯-KY 2025～2028F 各應用別營收占比

資料來源：公司資料及統一投顧預估

7. 中長期成長趨勢明確，投資評等調升至「強力買進」 
研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，隨 AI 機櫃電力與高速互
連規格持續升級，帶動單櫃供應產值提升，加上 Interplex 補足
精密機構能力，逐步擴大機櫃次系統供應範圍，有利深化客戶合作
關係並提升市占率。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建置不確定性升高，
可能使新平台導入及機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑市場評價，研究部將
本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍。以 2027 年稀釋後 EPS 145.24 
元為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至「強
力買進」。

附錄一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等 定義 
強力買進    預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上 
買進 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10%以上 
中立 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10% 
降低持股 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於(-10%)以下 
未評等 沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非

3Q26 Review: EV 판매 증가의 역풍 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 160,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 95,600 원(10/30)

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 31.6조원(+10.1%yoy), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.3%yoy, OPM 5.8%), 지배이익 1.29조원(-9.8%yoy)을 기록, 도매 판
매 증가로 인한 매출 증가에도 불구하고 인센티브/원자재 비용 증가로 인해 
컨센서스를 하회하는 실적 쇼크를 기록 
 영업이익 증감 요인: 인센티브&가격 -7,270억원, 믹스 -4,650억원, 재료비 
-3,680억원으로 총 1.56조원 비용 발생. 증가 요인은 판매량 +4,420억원, 
환율 +5,290억원, 기타비용 절감 +4,130억원으로 1.38조원 상쇄. 인센티
브로 인한 비용 증가 예상외로 컸는데 EV 판매량 증가 영향. 기말환율 하락
으로 판매보증충당금은 전년비 9,254억원 감소 
 전동화 믹스 증가: 글로벌 xEV 소매 판매 30만대(+47%yoy), 판매비중 
36.2%(+9.8%p yoy). HEV 17.2만대(+46%yoy), 텔루라이드, 스포티지 
HEV 공급 확대로 3분기 미국 HEV 판매 +118%yoy 기록. EV 12.3만대
(+75%yoy), 서유럽 EV 판매는 +163%yoy 기록 
 4분기 전망: 중동 분쟁 지속으로 원가 부담의 급격한 개선은 어려울 전망. 
연초 CID에서 제시한 연간 판매 목표는 달성 가능할 것으로 보이지만 기존 
수익성 목표를 하회할 전망. 중장기적으로 상품 경쟁력 강화, 지역별 파워트
레인 대응 전략 유지, 내부 원가 및 체질 개선으로 외부 비용 부담 상쇄를 
추진할 계획. 중동 분쟁 완화에 따른 비용 부담 해소와 HEV 비중 확대를 통
한 수익성 개선은 2027년 시점이 될 것으로 예상 
 단기 방향 전환 어려울 듯: 판매 호조에도 불구하고 저가형 EV 판매 비중 
증가함에 따라 수익성이 악화되는 모습이 3분기에도 재확인. 글로벌 수요 
변화를 감안했을 때, EV 시장 선점이 매우 중요한 것은 사실이지만 단기 수
익성 부담 커지고 있는 점 우려됨. 중국산 EV의 글로벌 확산, 중동 전쟁 장
기화에 따른 원가 부담 증대가 연말까지는 영향을 미칠 것. 동사에 대한 투
자의견 매수와 목표주가 16만원 유지함

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12 개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%) 
ㆍSTRONG BUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +50%이상 3% 
ㆍBUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +15%이상 ~ +50%미만 94% 
ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3% 
ㆍREDUCE(매도) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%미만 0%

STRONG RESILIENCE TO EXTERNAL SHOCKS
, 
SOLID 
MACROECONOMIC FUNDAMENTALS 
◼ 
 Over the past 
 two years
 , India has demonstrated remarkable resilience in the face of external shocks, while its 
macroeconomic fundamentals have remained solid.  
◼ 
 In FY2025/2026  
(ended in March 2026)
 , India was hit by a sharp rise in US tariffs. However, thanks to supportive fiscal 
and monetary policies, 
 real GDP  
grew by 7.8% in FY2026, placing India among the fastest
 -
 growing Asian economies.  
◼ 
 For the current fiscal year, the Indian economy is facing rising energy prices 
 (
due to 
 the conflict in the Middle East and 
increased food prices 
 because 
 of 
an 
unfavourable monsoon
 ) 
as well as 
 monetary tightening. Nevertheless, despite these 
new challenges, real GDP growth is expected to exceed 7%.  
◼ 
 India is currently in a stronger position than it was in 2022 to cope with the new energy shock. The fiscal leeway to support  
the economy has increased with the decline in the fiscal deficit, and inflation is more contained.  
◼ 
 Although 
 rising  
energy prices have put pressure on external accounts, FX reserves 
 remain  
sufficient to cover the country’s 
short
 -
 term external financing needs. 
 Yet, 
downward pressures on the rupee have been 
 significant 
 and 
could persist in the 
short term
 . 
In 
H1 
2026, India’s external accounts withstood the rise in energy prices fairly well
 , notably  
thanks to the sharp increase 
in workers’ remittances. 
 T
he 
widening of the trade deficit was primarily due to the substantial increase in machinery and 
equipment imports 
 while t
 he oil trade deficit increased 
 only 
moderately, as the rise in energy prices was offset by a decline 
in import volumes. 
 However, 
in H2 2026, the oil trade deficit is expected to widen significantly.  
Additionally, large portfolio outflows have exacerbated downward pressure on the rupee, necessitating interventions by 
the central bank in foreign exchange markets and the adoption of regulatory measures to support the currency. Although 
these measures hav
 e led to a rebound in the rupee (at least temporarily), inflationary pressures, volatile portfolio 
investments, and low net foreign direct investments are 
 expected to continue 
 to put pressure on the currency.  
◼ 
 D
ownward pressures on the rupee 
 and 
 rising bond yields will reduce the government's 
 already limited 
 fiscal capacity to 
increase public investment and 
 support economic development. However, this will not undermine its ability to refinance 
its debt, given its safe structure.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector is sufficiently robust to cope with the expected rise in credit risks.

STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH DESPITE 
STRUCTURAL CONSTRAINTS 
◼ 
 In October 2026, the IMF revised upward 
 India’s  
medium
 -
 term growth 
 forecast 
 from 6.5% to 6.8%  
per year
 . Nonetheless, 
to achieve such strong growth performance, 
 India will have to adapt to the challenges posed by artificial intelligence  
(AI)
, 
while its infrastructure and education system are not prepared for it.  
◼ 
 Moreover, India’s development remains highly uneven. 
 The middle class is still modest
 . This is explained by a large 
informal economy, heavy concentration of employment in low value
 -
 added sectors such as agriculture, low participation 
rates among women and youth, and limited industrial development. All this, in turn, reflects India’s weak i
 ntegration into 
global value chains.  
◼ 
 India faces two key structural challenges: i
 ) adapting the services sector to AI and ii) accelerating industrial development. 
Even with progress in sectors such as smartphones and pharmaceuticals, India’s industrial base remains too small to 
absorb its vast labour force. 
 Labo
u
r market 
 rigidities  
and the mismatch between training and business needs are holding 
back both the creation of quality jobs and industrial development.  
◼ 
 Low net foreign direct investment is a 
 major  
 obstacle. While 
 India attracts 
 new 
 FDI, retention is weak
 : 
 despite 
improvements in logistics, governance issues and regulatory barriers have 
 led 
 disinvestments to triple over the past five 
years.  
◼ 
 India 
 may fail in completing its 
 transition to a higher
 -
 value, more industrialised and inclusive economy if reforms do not 
accelerate 
 significantly
 . Structural change
 s 
could lag behind pressures from its rapidly growing population and 
 fast 
global 
technological advances
 . 
02 November 2026

### Page 3

EcoInsight

economic
 -
 research.bnpparibas.com

3

INDIA
’S COMPLEX POSITION 
IN INTERNATIONAL TRADE 
Low 
attractiveness  
due to unfavourable business environment and limited integration in global value chains  
◼ 
 Despite very strong growth, India's attractiveness is low relative to the size of its economy. The stock of foreign direct 
investment stood at only 15% of GDP in Q2 2026 compared to 52% of GDP in Vietnam and around 67% of GDP in Thailand 
and Malaysia.  
◼ 
 This lack of appeal can be attributed to 
 India’s still unfavo
 u
rable 
 business environment and 
 its 
 limited integration into 
global value chains. India’s global market share remain
 s 
 low (1.9% of total 
 world 
 exports  
 of goods
 )
, 
 especially in 
comparison with that of China (16.1% of total exports).  
◼ 
 India has not benefited from the AI boom
 , 
since its exports of AI
 -
 related goods remain modest (0.5% of its total exports)
 . 
However, 
 it has benefited from the “China +1” strategy adopted by 
 international 
 companies seeking to diversify their 
production sources. Smartphone exports by India have increased 6.3
 -
 fold over the past five years and accounted for almost 
6% of global exports in 2025 compared to less than 1% five year
 s 
earlier.  
To 
increase its a
 ttractiveness and develop its industrial sector, India has 
 multiplied F
 ree 
T
rade 
 A
greements  
◼ 
 Over the past 18 months, 
 India  
has signed a growing number of free trade agreements 
 – 
with the European Free Trade 
Association in October 2025, the United Kingdom in July 2025, Oman in December 2025, and the European Union in January 
2026
. This 
 should help to develop the industrial base and demonstrates the Modi government's commitment to 
accelerating India's integration into global trade.  
The sectors most likely to benefit from the recent trade agreement with the EU are electronics and semiconductors, 
pharmaceuticals and chemical
 s
, textile and renewable energy technologies. But expanding trade agreements without deep 
domestic improvements in the business environment, governance, education, and infrastructure will be ineffective. India 
will continue to struggle with investment reten
 tion. 
◼ 
 The US
 -
 India bilateral trade agreement has not yet been concluded. The two sides reached a framework for an interim 
Agreement in February 2026, under which the US would apply an 18% reciprocal tariff on Indian goods, with further 
reductions subject to the 
 successful conclusion of the agreement. As of early October, negotiations 
 are 
stalled. Key sticking 
points include market access, particularly 
 in 
India’s sensitive agricultural sector.

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE

Non transfer risk   
India’s external accounts are solid with a low external debt, moderate current account deficit and large FX 
reserves. 
 India’s net debtor position reached only 5.9% of GDP in Q2 2026. In the short term, external accounts 
are expected to deteriorate 
 given 
the rise in energy prices while downward pressures on the rupee should remain 
high.  
◼ 
 External accounts have remained solid 
 despite recent adverse shocks
 . 
 In 2025, external accounts remained solid 
despite the significant increase in US tariffs. In 
 H1 
 2026, they held up well against rising energy prices. Although the 
current account deficit is expected to widen for the current fiscal year as a whole, it should remain contained, and foreign 
exchange reserves should be more than sufficient to cover the co
 untry’s short
 -
 term financing needs. The 
 major  
causes 
for concern are the 
 structurally

portfolio investment, which exposes the currency to greater volatility.  
◼ 
 Structural vulnerability to energy price shock
 .

 oil and gas  
trade deficit is relatively high (2.8% of GDP in 2025). However, in H1 2026, the current account withstood fairly well the 
rise in energy prices especially thanks to the sharp increase in workers’ remittances  
(+0.7 pp to 3.7% of GDP)
 . The trade 
deficit widened significantly but principally because of the strong increase in machinery and equipment imports rather

Systemic credit risk  
The banking sector has strengthened over the past five years
 , 
as illustrated by the improvement in 
 asset  
quality 
and capital buffers. The main sources of credit risks are rising household debt, the rise in energy prices, the 
unfavourable monsoon and monetary tightening.  
◼ 
 The quality of assets has improved significantly. 
 In Q1 2026, the banking sector’s non
 -
 performing loan (NPL) ratio fell 
to a multi
 -
 decade low of 1.8%. For public banks  
(
54.4% of total loans
 )
, the NPL ratio declined to 2.1%.  
◼ 
 Capital buffers are strong. 
 While 
loan loss provisions remained modest at 75.6% of NPLs, capital buffers stayed strong. 
The Capital Adequacy Ratio (CAR) rose to 17.7% in Q1 2026, up from 17.4% a year earlier. From April 2027, Indian banks 
will adopt a new Expected Credit Loss (ECL) framewo
 rk, requiring earlier provisioning for potential loan losses. In the 
short term, higher provisioning requirements could push up lending rates and tighten credit conditions 
 while 
 triggering 
capital raising or targeted government recapitalisation. 
 I
n the medium term, the reform could strengthen banking  
sector 
resilience through earlier recognition of credit risks and more robust loss buffers.  
◼ 
 Banks’

 is 
 comfortable
 .

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 16-20
Date:    7 hours ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 16-20

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
November 16-20
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 14 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week’s inflation data ran hot. October’s headline CPI rose 0.4% m/m,
while the core CPI rose 0.3% for a third straight month. The PPI for final
demand rose 0.3% m/m in October after a revised 1.4% in September,
lifting the y/y rate to 6.5%, the highest since November 2022.
This week’s calendar is busy, with the FOMC minutes, Nvidia’s earnings,
Anthropic’s IPO, retail sales, and industrial production. Here’s more:
(1) FOMC minutes & FedSpeak. Minutes of the October 27-28 FOMC

meeting (Wed) should show how many of the 19 participants (12 voters
and 7 nonvoters) leaned toward a rate hike. The Committee held the
federal funds rate (FFR) target range at 3.75%-4.00% in a 10-2 vote, with
Fed Governor Lisa Cook and Cleveland Fed President Beth Hammack
dissenting in favor of a 25bps increase.
Six Fed officials speak this week (Warsh, Williams, Musalem, Hammack,
Goolsbee, and Paulson). Fed Chair Kevin Warsh appears Monday at the

Fed’s AI and productivity task force hearing in the San Francisco Bay
Area. New York Fed President John Williams, who speaks Monday and
Wednesday, said on Thursday that “inflation is far too high” and that
further tightening will be needed. We think the Fed should and will hike by
50bps in December.
FFR futures imply 3.6 rate hikes of 25 bps over the next 12 months
including 2.6 over the next six months (chart). The odds of a 50bps hike at
the December meeting are just 11.5% currently.

### Page 4

The 2-year Treasury bond yield is at 4.86%, well above the 4.00% top of
the FFR target range, with 12-month futures at 4.78% (chart).
(2) Nvidia earnings & Anthropic IPO. Nvidia reports Q3 earnings on
Tuesday, its first report since Jensen Huang’s resignation as CEO.
Revenue is expected to be $109.1 billion, up from $57.0 billion a year
earlier and slightly above the company’s $108 billion guidance. The
stock’s forward P/E is down to 11.5 (chart). The conference call with

interim CEO Colette Kress, who remains CFO, is likely to be more
revealing than the numbers.

### Page 5

Anthropic shares are expected to begin trading Thursday, with a price
range of $70-$78. That implies a fully diluted valuation of $1.26 trillion to
$1.40 trillion. We feel confident about the company’s prospects based on
its S-1 filing.
(3) Retail sales. October retail sales (Tue) follow September’s 0.8% m/m
gain, with sales ex autos up 0.9%. Retail sales ex food services rose 6.2%
y/y in September, and Redbook same-store sales continue to run well

ahead of the official measure (chart).
(4) Manufacturing. November’s NY and Philly Fed surveys (Mon, Thu)
follow solid October readings. Their average edged up to 23.2 in October
from 22.7 in September. The average has run well ahead of
manufacturing production growth this year, which was just 1.4% y/y in

### Page 6

September (chart). Historically, the two have tracked more closely,
suggesting that factory output could accelerate in the months ahead.
October’s industrial production (Tue) follows September’s 0.7% m/m gain,
which lifted the index to 104.0. Capacity utilization was 76.9%.
Manufacturing aggregate weekly hours slipped to 84.0 in October from
84.2 in September, suggesting that factory output weakened last month
(chart).
(5) ADP & unemployment claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading

(Tue) will update a four-week average that has been rising for the past two
weeks (chart).

### Page 7

Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 200,000, up from 189,000,
with the four-week average at 195,250 (chart). Continuing claims fell to
1,697,000 for the week ended October 30, with the four-week average
steady at 1,709,000.

Document 346: First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-18)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-18).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ labour market surveys, September quarter
2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 November 04 2026 Share
The unemployment rate rose to 5.7% in the September quarter, against expectations for a
small decline. Spare capacity in the labour market is keeping wage pressures subdued.
Unemployment rate: 5.7% (prev: 5.6%, Westpac: 5.5%, RBNZ: 5.5%, mkt: 5.5%)
Employment change: -0.1% (prev: +0.3%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.3%, mkt: +0.4%)

### Page 2

Participation rate: 70.4% (prev: 70.6%, Westpac: 70.8%, RBNZ: 70.6%, mkt: 70.7%) 
Labour costs (private sector): +0.5% (prev: +0.7%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.4%, mkt: +0.5%)

The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in New Zealand’s labour market. The
unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an almost five-year run of increases.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-
living pressures. 
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise 0.1% fall in employment in the
September quarter, after a downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That went against
the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward
momentum emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure had outpaced the MEI for the
previous nine quarters). It was also softer than suggested by the business-focused Quarterly
Employment Survey (QES), which recorded a 0.2% rise in full-time equivalent employees, a 0.1% rise
in filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. 
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in labour force participation rate, from 70.6%
to 70.4%. These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were indicative of sampling error in the survey. 
This is why we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, as it’s less vulnerable to
sampling error. And in that respect, the message on the jobs market remains weak: to the extent that
employment may be rising, it’s still not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population (which is
itself a fairly low bar, as net migration remains at a relatively low ebb). 
The HLFS also recorded a sharp 1.6% fall in hours worked (although usual hours worked were down
just 0.4%). This seems to have been influenced by Matariki – a floating holiday that was only
introduced a few years ago, so will not be picked up by the seasonal adjustment. This may have also
been responsible for a marked shift from full-time to part-time employment, as some people may have
been pushed into the part-time category simply by virtue of only working four days during the survey
week. 
The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter, in line with our expectations
overall but a bit weaker in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while there was a
surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector wages. There was a notable jump in the number of
employers citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay rates, although the size of
those increases varied a great deal, with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate. 
Overall, we think the details are soft from the RBNZ’s perspective – an important consideration ahead
of the 9 December Monetary Policy Statement, as there are few other major data releases between
now and then. The unemployment rate is a more reliable signal than the employment measures, and
the ongoing rise in unemployment points to more slack in the labour market than the RBNZ was hoping
to see in an economy that has been growing for over a year now. The doves on the policy committee
will be encouraged in their view that there is limited scope for headline inflation shocks to pass through
into second-round effects. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
OCTOBER CPI PREVIEW
Inflationary pressure fades as we move through year-end
17 November 2026
Key points
• October CPI to rise 0.41% with the annual 
pace lifting to 4.8%yr. The seasonally 
adjusted CPI lifts 0.7%mth, a small step 
up from the 0.6% increase in September.  
• October Trimmed Mean (TM) is set 
to gain 0.42%mth lifting the annual 
pace to 3.7%yr from 3.6%yr. 
• There is a small downside revision 
from our October 19th estimate for 
the December quarter CPI to 1.2%qtr 
while the TM remains 0.9%qtr.  
• The December quarter will represent the 
peak in Australian inflationary pressure. 
Our March quarter CPI remains 0.4%qtr 
and the TM is unchanged at 0.7%qtr. 
• We now see TM inflation dipping below 
3% by Q3 2027 and be well within the 
RBA’s target band by end 2027 at 2.7%yr.
• Demand remains the larger driver of 
inflation, but the recent pick-up in 
trimmed mean inflation appears to 
have been largely supply-driven.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Inflation accelerated in September...
The CPI rose 0.8% in the month of September, stronger than 
Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the market’s expectation of 
0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr. The 
seasonally adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting 
some positive seasonality in the month. 
Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: auto 
fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday travel & accommodation 
(8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation 
(7.2%mth); and property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There 
were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–3.1%mth); 
household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The more important underlying measure, the Trimmed Mean 
(TM), gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and 
market expectations with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr 
(revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised 
pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this 
series.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second 
consecutive month. The share of the basket running above 
3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, while the share below 2%yr 
rose from 27% to 32%. Meanwhile, our replication of the San 
Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum index moved into 
positive territory after trending higher since April. Around 
14% of the CPI basket, on an expenditure-weighted basis, 
experienced positive inflation shocks in September, driven 
by holiday travel, fuel, and clothing & footwear. This was 
partly offset by negative shocks across 7% of the basket, 
concentrated in food & non-alcoholic beverages. For full 
analysis of the September CPI, see “ Inflation accelerating in 
the run up to Christmas”.
... but is set to peak in October.
Looking ahead to October, early indicators were mixed. The 
NAB business survey suggested firms continued to absorb 
a meaningful share of higher input costs, with purchase cost 
growth edging up to 2.4%qtr (+0.1ppt from September), while 
retail price growth slowed sharply to 0.2%qtr (–0.8ppts), 
partly reflecting some moderation in labour cost growth 
(see here). Meanwhile, consumers’ year-ahead inflation 
expectations rose to 5.3% in October, with this rate holding 
steady in November. Fuel prices provided a more pronounced 
source of inflationary pressure, with retail petrol and diesel 
prices jumping 8.7% and 5.3%, respectively, in October to 
average $2.40/l and $2.82/l as global oil prices approached a 
local peak.
October to mark the peak in inflation
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
We expect the October CPI to rise 0.4% (0.41% to 2dp), lifting
annual inflation to 4.8%, which we expect to mark the peak.
On a seasonally adjusted basis, we estimate a stronger 0.7%
increase (0.68% to 2dp). For the monthly trimmed mean, we

expect a 0.4% rise (0.42% to 2dp), nudging the annual pace up
to 3.7%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking a 1.0% increase
in October. While conceptually different from the quarterly
trimmed mean, this measure nonetheless provides a useful
guide to near-term underlying inflation momentum. For market
services excluding volatiles & holiday travel, we have pencilled
in a 0.3% rise, lifting annual inflation to 4.0% from 3.8%,
which had marked a ten-month low.

Transport is expected to make the largest contribution
(+0.35ppts), almost entirely reflecting auto fuel (+0.32ppts).
Smaller contributions are expected from clothing & footwear
(+0.06ppts), household goods & services (+0.05ppts), and
meals out & takeaway (+0.04ppts). The main offset comes
from recreation & culture (–0.18ppts), driven entirely by
holiday travel (–0.18ppts), while fruit & vegetables, utilities
and several other components provide only marginal offsets.

Key details for the month of October
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to rise 0.2% in
October, lifting annual inflation to 3.3%. Strength in the month
is concentrated in meals out & takeaway, where we expect
prices to rise 0.6%mth and 4.3%yr. We have allowed for a
greater-than-usual degree of price resetting as businesses
adjust to the card surcharging ban.
Housing inflation is expected to rise 0.1% in October, with the
annual pace increasing to 6.2% reflecting base effects from

electricity. Across the major components:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.2%mth and 3.5%yr, with the
late-September indexation of the Commonwealth Rent
Assistance flowing through more broadly in October. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate is stronger at 0.4%mth.
• New dwelling inflation has been a key source of downside
surprise in recent months. We expect a 0.2%mth rise
in October, which would see the annual pace moderate
to 4.5%. The downturn in established home prices has

deepened into year-end (see here), which should weigh on
new dwelling inflation over the months ahead. Our current
estimates point to a trough of around 1.3%yr by late-2027.
• Maintenance & repair costs are expected rise a solid
0.9%mth, taking annual inflation to 3.5%, while gas & other
household fuels provide the main offset, falling 2.5%mth.
Transport is expected to be the main source of upward
pressure in October, rising 2.9%mth and lifting to 11.6%yr.

This largely reflects an 8.2%mth jump in auto fuel prices
following the rise in global oil prices through the month. Motor
vehicle prices and spare parts are also expected to increase, by
0.4%mth and 1.6%mth respectively.

Recreation & culture is expected to provide the key offset, 
falling 1.5%mth, driven by a 3.0%mth decline in holiday travel. 
We also expect some pull-back across components exposed 
to the AI trade, including audio, visual & computing equipment 
(–0.2%mth) and games, toys & hobbies (–0.3%mth).
Underlying inflation holds momentum
As noted above, our October TM estimate is also 0.4%mth, a step 
up from 0.3% in September taking the annual pace to 3.7%yr from 
3.6%yr and lifting the six month annualised pace to 4.2%yr, from 
4.0%yr, the fastest pace in the short history of the series. Rents 
and dwellings continue to underpin the TM estimates. 
Upside risks to October baseline ...
We view the risks around the October CPI outcome as slightly 
tilted to the upside. Our estimates already allow for a greater 
degree of price resetting in areas such as meals out & takeaway 
and personal and household services, but there is a risk that this 
adjustment proves broader than we have assumed. Increases in 
fuel levy surcharges in early October are another source of upside 
risk, including increases by Woolworths and Australia Post, with 
the latter nearly doubling its surcharge between September and 
October. The key downside risk remains housing, particularly 
new dwelling prices, which have surprised to the downside in 
recent months and could continue to do so as the housing market 
downturn deepens. Trade notifications data also point to some 
further downside risk, with builders on average announcing 
smaller price changes than in prior months.
... but momentum to ease in November ...
We have a preliminary estimate for the November CPI of 
–0.1%mth which will see the annual pace ease back to 4.6%yr. 
Auto fuel moved lower through November to be down –2.1% in 
the month with seasonal falls in garments, health, furniture & 
household appliances and holiday travel offsetting some strength 
in food, housing, communication and financial services. 
Seasonally adjusted the CPI is estimated to lift 0.1% in November, 
highlighting the seasonal weakness in the month, while our TM 
estimate is for a 0.2% lift seeing the annual pace ease a touch to 
3.6%yr while the six month annualised pace drops from 4.2%yr 
to 3.9%yr. This moderation is expected to continue into December 
with a preliminary TM estimate of 0.2%mth/3.6%yr. 
The following page has a table with monthly profiles for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to July 2027. By July 
2027 we now expect an annual pace of 3.3%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI while our estimate for the TM is 3.0%yr. 
More significantly, the six month annualised pace of the TM is 
estimated to be down to 2.7%yr by June 2027, well within the 
RBA’s target band. We have also included tables breaking down 
the monthly components of the CPI through the December and 
March quarters. 
... with December quarter now the peak ...
Our December quarter CPI estimate has been revised down 
slightly to 1.2%qtr, a modest 0.05ppt revision, with the annual 
pace lifting from 3.9%yr to 4.6%yr. The seasonally adjusted

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
CPI gains 1.4% in the December quarter highlighting normally
December is a seasonally soft quarter. Our TM estimate for
Q3 has a small downward revision of 0.05ppt to 0.85% at two
decimal place but this rounds up to our published estimate of
0.9%qtr. For now that suggests downside risk for our TM

estimate as getting the quarter to round to 0.8% is much
easier than getting it to round up to 1.0% . We also note
auto fuel prices falling faster than expected which may not
only result in downside risks to out CPI estimate but also our
TM estimate due to less pass through pressure.
... with inflation moderating through 2027 ...
Westpac’s preliminary March quarter CPI estimate is just 0.4%.
Driving most of this outcome is the 7.7% decline in autofuel

(–0.29ppt) and a 3.8% fall in holiday travel (–0.23ppt) being
offset by rising food (1.0%, 0.18ppt), housing (0.7%, 0.15ppt),
health (2.6%, 0.18ppt) and education (3.5%, 0.17ppt). The
March quarter does not have a lot in the way of net seasonally
with the seasonally adjusted estimate up 0.5%. Our underlying
TM measure of inflation is estimated to rise 0.7% in the March
quarter taking the annual pace down to 3.5% while the six
month annualised pace dips from 3.8%yr to 3.2%yr.

... as a disinflationary impulse kicks in.
We have for some time expected auto fuel prices to peak in
October, 240 cpl for petrol and 282 cpl for diesel, then to
trend lower from there. Our November estimates are currently
sitting on 237 cpl and 279 cpl respectively trending down to
186 cpl for petrol and 196 cpl for diesel by December 2027.
This represents a 12.7% fall in auto fuel through the first half
of 2027 subtracting 0.42ppt off the CPI over the same period

of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the
auto fuels profile please see: “Persistent energy pressures
have further to run”. At this stage we have not adjusted our
Brent forecast in line with the current correction underway
(see the November Market Outlook for more details why) as
we expect this to be a volatile ride. Should the current correc-
tion continue, auto fuel will represent a larger drag on the CPI
and potentially the Trimmed Mean.

Critical to our expected moderation in core inflation is a step
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab-
lished home prices. From a 1.0%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we
see dwelling price increasing by 0.4% in Q4 2026, and then
0.5% in Q1 and 0.4%qtr in Q2 before dropping to 0.2%qtr in
Q3 and Q4. This will see dwelling price inflation in the CPI
drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 2.3%yr by June 2027 then

down to 1.3%yr by December 2027.
At least to the end of this year there is still a high degree of
uncertainty about how much businesses will be able to pass
through upstream cost pressures. The latest NAB business sur-
vey continues to highlight robust input price pressure but also

that firms are struggling to pass on those increases.
For businesses, it is almost a now or never opportunity to raise
prices. We expect that through 2027, as energy prices ease
and the domestic economy slows under the weight of higher
interest rates and falling established home prices, households
will be much more resistant to price increases especially if dis-
cretionary household spending softens by more than expected.
That said, these headwinds may be at least partially offset by

the stronger starting point for household incomes, which has
proven more resilient than previously anticipated, and a possi-
ble bounce back in energy prices due to renewed conflict in the
Middle East.
Supply or demand driven inflation?
Inflation has steadily drifted away from the RBA’s target over
recent quarters. While the demand side is the larger driver of
inflation currently, most of the recent pick-up in TM inflation
appears to be from the supply side, and the more detailed

breakdown may understate how much.
There are several ways to split inflation into supply and de-
mand. One is to estimate how much of the rise a standard de-
mand-driven model can explain and attribute the rest to supply
(see here). Run through to Q3 2026, this approach puts most of
the recent pick-up in TM inflation down to supply. Without it,
trimmed mean would have risen from around 3.0%yr to 3.2%yr
over the year to Q3 2026, rather than the 2.9%yr to 3.7%yr

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
rise we actually saw. The supply contribution is moderate,
though, and well below its Covid-era peak. This analysis also
reveals that most of the disinflation through late-2023 and into
early-2025 reflected supply pressures unwinding, while de-
mand-driven inflation remained stickier, persisting above 3%yr.

Another is the San Francisco Fed approach, which the RBA has
also replicated (see here). The attribution is that if prices rise
and quantities fall, it is supply; if both rise, it is demand. We run
it to Q2 2026, as Q3 volume data are not yet available. Like the
first approach, it finds that demand accounts for most of infla-
tion (headline in this case). However, unlike the first approach,
it shows supply contributions easing a little.
A closer look at the categories classified as demand tells a

more nuanced story. Utilities, health and insurance all feature,
and their prices are largely shaped by government policy or are
not the categories that usually come to mind when people talk
about demand-driven inflation, and they are not very sensitive
to interest rates. You can look at electricity as a clear example
where prices in recent history have been set by government
policies (the introduction of, and then removal of, cost of living
rebates as well as policies to decarbonise the economy) or ex-

ternal supply shock (rising gas prices pushing up the marginal
cost of generation).
Categories more commonly thought of discretionary do show
up on the demand side, with recreation & culture (excluding
travel) and meals out & takeaway being the key ones. The
drawback of this sort of decomposition is that it labels cate-
gories where prices and quantities move together as demand,
even when this may reflect the ripple effects of a supply shock
elsewhere, a point our Chief Economist Luci Ellis detailed in a

note in 2024 (see In a world gone bananas, don’t squash the
other fruit). Some of this may be occurring now. In particular,
the analysis is likely not capturing the shift in spending towards
these categories resulting from travel disruptions associated
with the Middle East conflict (travel services itself was classi-
fied as a supply-side contributor in Q2). As travel normalises,
some of the “demand” contribution from recreation & culture
and meals out & takeaway should therefore fade over coming

quarters.
We would also acknowledge the work of Tim Toohey and his
analysis “The RBA has an identity problem”.

Charts of the week: Watching earnings and a potential ‘Blue wave’

Editorial 
Policy, politics and earnings  
Strategy summary: We retain our near-term defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, sectors and regions amid 
near-term uncertainty around monetary policy, politics and 
earnings. The next two weeks will be busy for major central 
banks, US political watchers and technology leaders powering 
the AI supercycle. We expect the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold 
rates next week, but markets will focus on whether central 
banks are turning more hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices. The US mid-term elections are expected to give 
Democrats control of the House and Senate, with implications 
for taxes and the AI-led investment boom.  
Meanwhile, the consensus is expecting the US tech sector to 
report over 60% y/y earnings growth for Q3, the biggest jump 
in over two decades outside the post-COVID rebound.  Each of 
these three drivers have the potential to surprise markets.  
Big Three to hold monetary policy: We expect the Fed, ECB 
and BoJ to hold policy rates next week, after September’s 
hikes, as they assess whether higher bond yields have 
tightened financial conditions enough to offset fuel-price-driven 
inflation. We expect the Big Three to hike next in December as 
fuel prices are likely to stay elevated into early next year due to 
refining capacity constraints. Nevertheless, the Fed and ECB 
are likely to deliver less than around 60bps of rate hikes priced 
in by money markets over the next 12 months on the back of 
an eventual easing of oil and fuel prices leading to disinflation 
by H2 2027. This outlook means 3-7-year Developed Market 
investment grade bonds currently offer attractive risk-reward.  
Opportunity in Australian bonds. Other major central banks, 
such as the Bank of England, Bank of Canada and Reserve 
Bank of New Zealand are likely to hike rates by 25bps in the 
next two weeks given their focus on taming near-term inflation. 
However, we believe the Reserve Bank of Australia (RBA) is 
close to the end of its hiking cycle, even if it hikes one last time 
in the coming months (money markets give a 60% chance of a 
hike on 3 November). Australia’s domestic labour and housing 
markets and business confidence are softening after the RBA 
hiked its policy rate four times this year to a 15-year high. 
Meanwhile, Australia’s cash rate is 60bps higher than the US 
policy rate, supporting the AUD. We continue to see attractive 
income and FX gain opportunities from AUD corporate bonds.  
A ‘Blue Wave’ in the US? Prediction markets strongly favour 
Democrats taking the House of Representatives and possibly 
the Senate in the 3 November US mid-term elections. While an 
opposition sweep in the mid-terms has historically delivered the 
best 12-month returns for equities among all scenarios, partly 
due to the resulting policy gridlock, a decisive Democratic “blue 
wave” could increase longer-term risks of higher taxes and 
uncertainty over policy towards AI investment and data centres. 
With the Senate race still close and prediction markets fallible, 
we remain cautious on risk assets in the near term.  
Closing EU bank contingent convertible bonds (CoCos1) 
and US 10-year government bond ideas: We lock in 2% 
gains in EU CoCos and 1.7% gain in US 10-year government 
bond ideas amid French and US political risks (page 5-6). We 
continue to hedge any inflation risks through inflation-protected 
bonds and geopolitical risk through an Overweight in gold.  
Watching Big Tech earnings:  Upcoming Big Tech earnings 
raises uncertainty as market leadership remains concentrated 
and share-price reactions more mixed. With valuations less 
likely to drive returns, earnings delivery and sustainability of AI 
investment, funding and monetisation and broadening out of 
earnings beyond tech will be critical, raising the risk of volatility 
and sector divergence. We remain invested but defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification across sectors and regions.  
Staying defensive and diversified. We recently upgraded the 
defensive US Healthcare sector to Overweight, while retaining 
our Overweights to US tech and materials as well as financials 
in the US, Euro area and Japan. We also downgraded the more 
volatile Communications Services sector to Neutral and took 
profits on the semiconductor segment earlier this month before 
the latest correction. Our defensive and diversified stance 
should preserve participation in further equity upside, while 
reducing exposure to earnings disappointments and supporting 
resilience through the reporting season.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the key takeaways from the technology sector 
earnings reported so far this week? 
Our view: Recent technology sector earnings results point to 
strong compute demand and continued capex expansion, 
supporting our positive long-term view on the AI investment 
theme. However, rising funding and execution risks warrant 
near-term caution. We prefer to avoid increasing exposure to 
higher-risk areas within the technology sector for now. 
Rationale: Earnings results reported this week suggest major tech 
companies remain committed to expanding their AI infrastructure. 
While this bodes well for AI infrastructure providers, ambitious 
expansion plans imply that an increasing share of AI capex is likely 
to depend on debt financing. We expect debt-financed AI capex to 
rise from c.25% of AI capex in 2026 to c.40% in 2027.  
Greater reliance on borrowing increases sensitivity to interest rates, 
while deployment delays could postpone revenue recognition amid 
ongoing financing costs. However, evidence of improving AI 
monetisation and adoption should strengthen operating cash 
flows and support internal capex funding. Against this backdrop, 
we will assess Big Tech earnings next week for greater clarity on 
capex guidance and funding and AI monetisation trends.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Euro area bank equities amid 
heightened political and fiscal uncertainty in the region? 
Our view: We remain positive on Euro area bank equities, 
supported by resilient earnings and shareholder-friendly capital 
return policies. While France’s fiscal uncertainty and a softer 
growth outlook warrant caution, the recent pullback in Euro 
area bank equities offers selective opportunities. We favour 
well-capitalised banks with strong deposit franchises and 
diversified earnings. 
Rationale: We expect Euro area banks’ Q3 2026 net interest 
income to grow 1.5-3.0% q/q, led by Spanish banks, driven by 
higher rates and continued asset repricing. Resilient fee income from 
wealth management and investment banking activity, as well as 
dividends and buybacks, should provide further support. Price-to-
book (P/B) valuations have eased, despite expectations for 
healthy return on equity (ROE), thus improving risk-reward. 
Fiscal uncertainty in France has widened sovereign spreads, 
pressuring banks’ bond holdings and funding costs. Although 
French banks represent 13% of the Euro area Bank Index, volatility 
could spread to regional financial markets. However, stress in the 
broader banking system appears limited, supported by strong 
capital buffers and ECB tools to contain disorderly market moves and 
sovereign market contagion. We will continue to monitor credit 
trends, sovereign spreads and banks’ 2027 earnings guidance. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What has driven the recent weakness in China equities, 
and what is your outlook from here? 
Our view: We maintain China equities as a Core holding, as 
valuations remain inexpensive. Rapid technological innovation 
and investment in AI localisation provide tailwinds, but the 
potential for a re-rating appears delayed. Investors should seek 
to diversify their portfolios and reduce concentration risk. 
Rationale: China equities have weakened, driven by mounting 
scepticism over AI capex returns, structural margin 
compression and concerns about the country’s significant 
financing requirements. Another notable feature in China has been 
significant share issuance, which has diluted earnings per share and 
weighed on the MSCI China Index’s performance. These pressures 
have been compounded by geopolitical headwinds, restrictions on 
advanced chips, an enduring property sector contraction and weak 
domestic demand. September’s stimulus measures also 
disappointed markets in terms of their scale and pace of delivery. 
Notwithstanding these headwinds, valuations remain inexpensive, 
supported by rapid domestic innovation and structural tailwinds 
in AI localisation, leading us to retain China equities as a Core 
holding. Elevated volatility reinforces the need to be defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification rather than relying on technology 
sector concentration. We also remain Overweight China’s 
healthcare, financial and materials sectors. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Are you still bullish on US 10-year government bonds?   
Our view: We are closing our opportunistic bullish idea on US 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. The risk-reward 
has become less compelling after the yield broke below 5%, the 
lower bound of our three-month forecast range of 5.00-5.25%. 
Rationale: The US 10-year yield fell over 17bps to 4.97% on 22 
October – its first move below 5% since 23 September and biggest 
one-day drop since March 2023. A sell-off in AI-related equities and 
renewed Middle East tensions drove investors towards safe havens. 
Earlier this month, the yield hit its highest level in over two decades. 
The forces behind the rally are fragile, though. Oil prices remain 
elevated amid escalating Middle East tensions, and September US 
consumer inflation rose to 3.7% y/y. Fed officials have signalled 
there is no urgency to raise rates at the 27-28 October meeting, 
but persistent price pressures keep further tightening on the table. 
We prefer to lock in gains and close our opportunistic bullish idea on 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO, Fixed Income & FX

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for French bank bonds, especially 
ahead of France’s upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews?   
Our view: The recent sell-off in French bank bonds is a repricing 
of tail risks, not a credit story. We expect further price volatility 
until the April 2027 French presidential election, particularly at 
the junior end of the capital structure, so we are closing our 
opportunistic EU bank contingent convertibles (CoCos1) idea at 
a gain of 2.0%. 
Rationale: We believe the recent sell-off in French bank bonds 
reflects domestic political and fiscal risks rather than weaker 
credit fundamentals. The sustained widening of the OAT-Bund 
spread has raised concerns about the profit and loss (P&L) impact 
on banks' sovereign debt holdings. Banks provide limited 
disclosure on these exposures. However, banks’ investment 
portfolios are generally diversified, and holdings booked at amortised 
cost should soften the P&L impact. Near-term catalysts include the 
upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews. A sovereign downgrade 
could trigger bank rating downgrades, with domestically focused 
lenders potentially more exposed than regionally diversified peers. A 
sovereign credit event is not our base case, but we expect headline 
risks to persist until the French presidential election. Against this 
backdrop, we are closing our opportunistic EU bank CoCos idea. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Will the November US mid-term elections and European 
bond volatility support a sustained Swiss franc (CHF) rebound?  
Our view: We expect a near-term CHF rebound, supported 
mainly by safe-haven demand amid European bond volatility. 
We believe the US mid-term elections will be a secondary driver. 
Rationale: European sovereign bond volatility provides the stronger 
catalyst for CHF strength. The France-Germany government bond 
yield spread remains elevated near 160bps, reflecting heightened 
French fiscal concerns and reinforcing the CHF’s safe-haven appeal. 
The US mid-terms could provide additional support for the CHF. 
A Democratic victory in either chamber is expected to constrain 
further fiscal expansion, potentially weighing on the USD. However, 
this would not necessarily lead to faster fiscal consolidation. 
The SNB’s 0% policy rate leaves the CHF at a sizeable interest-
rate disadvantage vs. the USD, constraining sustained CHF gains. 
With EUR/CHF below 0.90, excessive CHF strength could also 
increase the risk of SNB intervention. A decisive USD/CHF break 
below 0.8130 would strengthen the technical case for a more 
sustained CHF rebound.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

A widening France-Germany 10-year government 
bond yield spread and a stable investment-grade 
(IG) bond option-adjusted spread (OAS) suggest a 
repricing of political and fiscal risk rather than 
credit risk

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would worsening budget deficits and political uncertainty 
across parts of Europe affect your view on EUR/USD and Euro 
area government bond yields? 
Our view: Fiscal and political concerns constrain near-term 
EUR/USD gains, with France being the primary vulnerability. We 
expect EUR/USD to recover towards the 1.17-1.18 range over 
the next 12 months, supported by continued Euro area 
economic growth and an expected fading of USD support. We 
expect wider differentials in Euro area government bond yields, 
with political risk premium elevated in French government 
bonds (OATs) and German government bonds (Bunds) 
supported by safe-haven demand. 
Rationale: France's high public debt, large budget deficit and 
political obstacles to fiscal adjustments are weighing on market 
confidence, with the country’s 10-year government bond yield 
rising above 5%. The risk of stress spreading to Italy and Spain 
adds to regional concerns, although current evidence does not 
establish a broad European fiscal crisis. German government bonds 
should benefit from safe-haven demand, while fiscal concerns keep 
risk premia on vulnerable countries' bonds elevated. EUR/USD 
remains more sensitive to US-Germany interest-rate differentials 
and broader market confidence than to French bond yields alone. 
France’s fiscal risks, elevated energy costs and high US interest 
rates weigh on the EUR in the near term. Over the next 12 months, 
Germany’s multi-year public investment programme, resilient 
exports and sound banking-sector fundamentals are expected to 
support Euro area growth. We expect the Fed's rate-hiking cycle to 
peak by mid-2027, reducing USD support and driving the EUR  
towards 1.17-1.18 . 
The principal tail risk is fiscal stress widening government bond 
yield spreads and spilling over into bank funding conditions and 
lending activity, weakening growth and delaying the EUR's recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

France’s fiscal risks constrain near-term EUR/USD 
gains, but a modest recovery remains likely
